name=\"offerings_update[body]\" id=\"offerings_update_form_2537_offerings_update_body\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eDear CallingDr followers,\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eKingscrowd\u003c/strong\u003e, is a company that was founded with the vision that everyone should have access to institutional grade research and analytics tools that enable informed startup investment decisions, regardless of investment experience, as listed on their website.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThey chose CallingDr as the Deal of the month, as listed in this article. \u003cem\u003e(note, you might need subscription to read the article. CallingDr is not affiliated with Kingscrowd in anyway or form).\u003c/em\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.kingscrowd.com/deal-to-watch-callingdr/#mepr_jump\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003ehttps://www.kingscrowd.com/deal-to-watch-callingdr/#mepr_jump\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eKingscrowd talks about slow adoption of Telehealth in USA, \u003cem\u003e\"The reasons for the slow adoption of telehealth are myriad. However, there’s widespread agreement about some of the major factors. One is the \u003ca href=\"https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/tech/only-1-10-patients-use-telehealth-as-lack-awareness-hinders-adoption-j-d-power-survey-finds\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003elack of awareness\u003c/a\u003e among prospective telemedicine patients. Another issue is the lack of affordability, particularly given the \u003ca href=\"https://www.kff.org/womens-health-policy/issue-brief/opportunities-and-barriers-for-telemedicine-in-the-u-s-during-the-covid-19-emergency-and-beyond/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003elimited reimbursement coverage\u003c/a\u003e of telehealth by major insurance payers like Medicare. The final major hurdle is the \u003ca href=\"https://www.medpagetoday.com/meetingcoverage/acp/79180\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003ehigh outlays\u003c/a\u003e (in terms of financial cost, time, and clinician buy-in) needed to integrate telehealth offerings into existing care workflows.\"\u003c/em\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThen they move to the solution provided by CallingDr,\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch4\u003e\u003cem\u003e\"The Solution\u0026nbsp;\u003c/em\u003e\u003c/h4\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cem\u003eCallingDr is a telehealth enablement company that is providing multiple digital pathways for patients and providers to connect:\u003c/em\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cem\u003eThe core CallingDr offering is a Platform-as-a-Service (PaaS) solution that provides clinics with a comprehensive set of features and capabilities for deploying telehealth services. These features include secure messaging, remote monitoring, self-pay \u0026amp; co-pay options, and ordering of prescriptions and labs. The platform makes it easy for clinics to integrate and offer telehealth to their patients.\u003c/em\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cem\u003eComplementing the core platform is the FindingDr service. It operates as an online appointment-booking service that patients can use to find suitable physicians and facilities. FindingDr lets patients book visits (both in-person and telemedicine appointments) via a few quick taps on their phone.\u003c/em\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cem\u003eFinally, the company also offers “CallingDr OnDemand.” This is a pool of available physicians (covering Primary Care as well as specialties such as Pediatrics and Behavioral Health), so that patients with acute needs can match with providers for immediate virtual care.\"\u003c/em\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003cp\u003eTalk in detail about the team and management:\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cem\u003e\"The Team\u0026nbsp;\u003c/em\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cem\u003eCallingDr, and its parent company MyApps Corporation, are led by CEO Adnan Malik, an experienced technologist with a multi-decade career spanning major IT companies such as Seagate, Veritas Software, and Symantec. Prior to founding MyApps, he most recently served as CTO at an electronic medical records company. While there he created the firm’s ambulatory EMR platform, taking it through full certification as well as integration with major lab test and pharmacy providers.\u003c/em\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cem\u003eThe current CTO of CallingDR, Kashif Akram, also has deep experience with EMR creation, having previously worked at two different EMR providers.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/em\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cem\u003eCallingDr’s corporate and advisory boards also feature multiple experienced physicians and healthcare providers who bring a variety of specializations to bear, ranging from pulmonology and cardiology to physiotherapy and family medicine.\"\u003c/em\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThen talk about growth, potential market share, and finally, why they like CallingDr?\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cem\u003e\"Partnering With Clinics Rather Than Trying To Sell To Patients – Although the rates of telehealth adoption prior to the COVID-19 pandemic were underwhelming, there were still some winners pulling away from the rest of the pack as of 2019. These emerging leaders included Teladoc (a \u003ca href=\"https://www.marketwatch.com/investing/stock/tdoc\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003epublic company\u003c/a\u003e with $14B market cap) and Doctor on Demand (a private company with unicorn aspirations, having raised at a \u003ca href=\"https://www.crunchbase.com/organization/doctor-on-demand\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003enine-figure valuation\u003c/a\u003e in 2018).\u0026nbsp;\u003c/em\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cem\u003eThe CallingDr team has differentiated themselves by focusing on clinic partnerships, rather than trying to get individual patients to pay for a telehealth service. In contrast to services like Teladoc and Doctor on Demand (which \u003ca href=\"https://www.safesmartliving.com/health/online-doctors/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003echarge patients $50 and up\u003c/a\u003e for individual appointments), CallingDr doesn’t charge patients anything. Instead, it monetizes by charging clinics or individual physicians for their use of the platform as a means to connect with their existing patients. This differentiation makes it much more likely that any given patient will be willing to use – and keep using – the CallingDr app.\u003c/em\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cem\u003eSubscription Revenues Are The Key To Investors’ Hearts – From the standpoint of assessing CallingDr as an investment, its choice of a subscription-based business model would be regarded by \u003ca href=\"https://www.zuora.com/guides/three-reasons-wall-street-loves-recurring-revenue-models/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003emany prospective investors\u003c/a\u003e as one of the attractive aspects of this opportunity. Subscription plans create streams of recurring cash flow that make a company’s revenue much more \u003ca href=\"https://seekingalpha.com/article/4266314-wanna-reduce-risk-invest-in-recurring-revenue\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003estable and predictable\u003c/a\u003e than non-recurring business models. The company’s current subscription pricing of between $50 and $150 monthly per physician would likely represent a very reasonable cost proposition to hospitals and clinics.\"\u003c/em\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eOverall a good resource to read as a whole article. thanks\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/textarea\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv id=\"offerings_update_form_2503_offerings_update_body\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eRemote patient monitoring (RPM) uses digital technologies to collect medical and other forms of health data such as \u0026nbsp;vital signs, weight, blood pressure, blood sugar, blood oxygen levels, heart rate, and electrocardiograms from individuals in one location (most probably from homes or offices) and electronically transmit that information securely to health care providers in a different location (such as Centers, Clinics, Practices and Hospitals). The main idea is to stop the patient from coming back to hospitals and avoid readmissions.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eCallingDr remote patient monitoring system helps population stay healthy, allows older population and patients with disabilities to stay and live at homes rather than check in to skilled Nursing Homes as well as avoid hospitalizations. When using CallingDr, the patients can be assured that all the information including their vitals as well as PHI (patient health information), can be transmitted real-time to their physicians of choice, and hence help them to visit clinics offices regularly.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eNot only the patients get all the help they need at the comfort of their homes, but physicians and clinics can monitor their patients in real-time, and if needed do a quick consult with a click of a button.\u003cbr\u003eThis is recognized by CMS as an essential tool, as government is willing to \u0026nbsp;reimburse doctors for patient monitoring and care, as they devote time to help their patients.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eCallingDr platform, is a win-win for both patients and doctors.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003ePlease help us get the word out to your doctors and communities, to adopt CallingDr as RPM platform of choice.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eRegards,\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/textarea\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class='s-marginTop1_5 s-marginPullHoriz1 s-paddingHoriz1 s-borderTop1 The challenge of having enough doctors to service our communities will get even worse as the nation's population continues to grow and age.\"\u0026nbsp;\u003c/em\u003ePeople to efficiently connect with doctors, despite the growing physician shortage issue.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u0026nbsp;CallingDr’s comprehensive telemedicine solution mitigates the impact of physician shortage, allowing patients to be connected with doctors anywhere anytime if accessing local doctors becomes increasingly challenging.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u0026nbsp;CallingDr not only significantly increases the productivity of health workers but also makes patients' lives easier. id=\"offerings_update_form_2388_offerings_update_body\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eDear Share holders and CallingDr Followers,\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eI have been getting some messages on the status of overall market when it comes to telemedicine. And about the competition and healthy growth opportunities in this field or not.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eI want to start by saying, that Telemedicine and Telehealth market is wide open. The adoption last year in healthcare sector was at 1%, with 99% wide open to capture. With the COVID-19 pandemic, the opportunity opened up tremendously and lots of players came into the market to start selling tele-visit products. Some of the products what I call were genuine products, based on their R\u0026amp;D and resources but multiple companies, contacted other established companies and got the product \"white labelled\". This is where we stand out amongst others. Here is a little more on our company and products:\u003c/p\u003e\u003col\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u0026nbsp;We have our own native products, and have native apps, that run on IOS, Android and WebRTC. We do not license our products, but have our own R\u0026amp;D develop these in-house.\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eSince we have genuine products, we white label our technologies to others who need it. For example, if a doctor office wants a specialized version with their logos and branding, we can make one for them, to run on all platforms. Hence more revenue for the company.\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eCallingDr is one of the products the company offers, and is our flagship product, but we have others like VisitingDr and FindingDr. In addition to this, being a health tech company we can do custom \u0026nbsp;healthcare projects with enhancements and integrations.\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ol\u003e\u003cp\u003eWhen you are investing, you are NOT investing in one product, but a technology company that has multiple projects in the works. You can read the features of our flagship product, in the pitch area, but I wanted to share this with you all.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThanks for your
